[0m[1m[38;5;9merror[vulnerability][0m[1m: Data from an abandoned stream leaks into the next stream on a reused context[0m
    [0m[36m┌─[0m /home/runner/work/chump/chump/Cargo.lock:774:1
    [0m[36m│[0m
[0m[36m774[0m [0m[36m│[0m [0m[31mzstd 0.13.3 registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index[0m
    [0m[36m│[0m [0m[31m━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━[0m [0m[31msecurity vulnerability detected[0m
    [0m[36m│[0m
    [0m[36m├[0m ID: RUSTSEC-2026-0391
    [0m[36m├[0m Advisory: https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0391
    [0m[36m├[0m The `with_context` constructors of the stream encoders and decoders used the
      borrowed context as it was. When a stream was abandoned on a context, for
      example an encoder dropped before `finish()` after an error, whatever it
      left there was carried into the next stream: the input buffered by the
      previous encoder (up to 128 KiB, or all of it with `zstdmt`) became the
      start of the next frame, and a decoder returned undelivered output of the
      previous stream.
      
      An application reusing one context for data that must stay separate, such
      as different users' requests, could disclose one user's plaintext to
      another. Whoever supplies the data can cause the abandonment with invalid
      input.
      
      The flaw was corrected by resetting the context's session in
      `with_context`.
    [0m[36m├[0m Announcement: https://github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/commit/74ace2dcdca62a2ce0f034895d6079458311e003
    [0m[36m├[0m Solution: Upgrade to >=0.14.3 (try `cargo update -p zstd`)

[0m[36m775[0m [0m[36m│[0m [0m[31mzstd-safe 7.2.4 registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index[0m
    [0m[36m│[0m [0m[31m━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━[0m [0m[31msecurity vulnerability detected[0m
    [0m[36m│[0m
    [0m[36m├[0m ID: RUSTSEC-2026-0398
    [0m[36m├[0m Advisory: https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0398
    [0m[36m├[0m `ZSTD_seekable_decompress` shortens the requested length to the end of the
      data without checking that the offset is before it. For an offset past the
      end, it returns a wrapped-around length without writing anything, and
      `Seekable::decompress` passed that length to `Vec::set_len`.
      
      With the `seekable` feature, whoever supplies the archive, or chooses the
      offset, can leave the caller's `Vec<u8>` with a length far beyond its
      capacity. Reading it then discloses neighbouring heap memory, and appending
      to it corrupts memory. Offsets near `u64::MAX` can also hang the call.
      
      The flaw was corrected by clamping requests to the end of the data, and by
      checking every count reported by the C library against the buffer capacity.
    [0m[36m├[0m Announcement: https://github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/commit/80506be743fae2ed0c2fe8b21c374c6a2eab0be7
    [0m[36m├[0m Solution: Upgrade to >=8.2.0 (try `cargo update -p zstd-safe`)
    [0m[36m├[0m zstd-safe v7.2.4
      └── zstd v0.13.3
          └── wasmtime-internal-cache v49.0.1
              └── wasmtime v49.0.1

[0m[1m[38;5;9merror[vulnerability][0m[1m: Uninitialized memory exposed when streaming decompression of a legacy frame fails[0m
    [0m[36m┌─[0m /home/runner/work/chump/chump/Cargo.lock:775:1
    [0m[36m│[0m
[0m[36m775[0m [0m[36m│[0m [0m[31mzstd-safe 7.2.4 registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index[0m
    [0m[36m│[0m [0m[31m━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━[0m [0m[31msecurity vulnerability detected[0m
    [0m[36m│[0m
    [0m[36m├[0m ID: RUSTSEC-2026-0403
    [0m[36m├[0m Advisory: https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0403
    [0m[36m├[0m When decompression fails inside a legacy (zstd v0.4 to v0.7) frame, the
      bundled zstd library reports the output buffer as fully written although
      nothing was written. `DCtx::decompress_stream` committed that position even
      on error, and for a `Vec<u8>` destination this extended the vector's length
      over uninitialized memory.
      
      Legacy support is enabled by default. A 5- or 6-byte corrupt legacy frame is
      enough: through `zstd::stream::write::Decoder`, up to 32 KiB of stale heap
      memory is then passed to the wrapped writer on the next `write()` or
      `flush()`, or when an `auto_flush()`/`on_flush()` decoder is dropped. Whether
      this reaches an attacker depends on where the application sends the
      decompressed output. `zstd::stream::read::Decoder`, `zstd::decode_all` and
      the bulk API are not affected.
      
      Before zstd-safe 4.0.0, output buffers could only be initialized slices.
      The zstd crate's own writer had the same flaw from 0.4.22 to 0.7.x, which is
      covered by a separate advisory.
      
      The flaw was corrected by only committing the buffer positions of a
      successful call.
    [0m[36m├[0m Announcement: https://github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/commit/006e1d1ad1eeaf6b5d4c28329e70217adabcec43
    [0m[36m├[0m Solution: Upgrade to >=8.2.0 (try `cargo update -p zstd-safe`)
    [0m[36m├[0m zstd-safe v7.2.4
      └── zstd v0.13.3
          └── wasmtime-internal-cache v49.0.1
              └── wasmtime v49.0.1
                  ├── chump v0.2.0
                  ├── wasmtime-wasi v49.0.1
                  │   └── chump v0.2.0 (*)
                  ├── wasmtime-wasi-io v49.0.1
                  │   └── wasmtime-wasi v49.0.1 (*)
                  └── wiggle v49.0.1
                      └── wasmtime-wasi v49.0.1 (*)

[0m[1m[38;5;9merror[vulnerability][0m[1m: WebAssembly allocator shim corrupts memory on allocation failure[0m
    [0m[36m┌─[0m /home/runner/work/chump/chump/Cargo.lock:776:1
    [0m[36m│[0m
[0m[36m776[0m [0m[36m│[0m [0m[31mzstd-sys 2.0.16+zstd.1.5.7 registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index[0m
    [0m[36m│[0m [0m[31m━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━[0m [0m[31msecurity vulnerability detected[0m
    [0m[36m│[0m
    [0m[36m├[0m ID: RUSTSEC-2026-0410
    [0m[36m├[0m Advisory: https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0410
    [0m[36m├[0m On wasm32, zstd-sys provides `malloc` and `free` to the C library through a
      shim over Rust's allocator. The shim did not check for allocation failure:
      it wrote its size header to address 0 and returned address 4, which the C
      library used as a valid buffer. `free(NULL)` also read below address 0.
      
      Whenever the instance cannot grow its memory by what a frame asks for, a
      small crafted frame (a 9-byte header declaring a 128 MiB window is enough)
      made decompression write decoded data over the bottom of the module's
      linear memory, instead of failing. The corruption stays within the wasm
      sandbox.
      
      The flaw was corrected by returning NULL on allocation failure or size
      overflow, and by making `free(NULL)` a no-op.
    [0m[36m├[0m Announcement: https://github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/commit/165d1625553c236b9700ca794b8a7c51f5d98314
    [0m[36m├[0m Solution: Upgrade to >=2.1.2 (try `cargo update -p zstd-sys`)
    [0m[36m├[0m zstd-sys v2.0.16+zstd.1.5.7
      └── zstd-safe v7.2.4
          └── zstd v0.13.3
              └── wasmtime-internal-cache v49.0.1
                  └── wasmtime v49.0.1

                      ├── wasmtime-wasi v49.0.1
                      │   └── chump v0.2.0 (*)
                      ├── wasmtime-wasi-io v49.0.1
                      │   └── wasmtime-wasi v49.0.1 (*)

                          └── wasmtime-wasi v49.0.1 (*)
